# 01-pastry-kitchen-bakery-case-opening-closing-checklists.xlsx
# docProps: title='Pastry Kitchen & Bakery Case Opening and Closing Checklists' · subject='Pastry Shop Checklists Kit Pro · v2.0' · keywords='pastry shop checklist, bakery opening checklist, bakery cleaning schedule, cake order form, bakery production schedule, bakery allergen chart, food temperature log, receiving log, AI Chef Pro' · description='aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates' · category='AI Chef Pro · Digital products' · creator='AI Chef Pro'

=== Hoja «Instructions» · protegida=False · área= · títulos=None · paneles=None · papel=1 · orientación=portrait
B2     : 01 · Pastry Kitchen & Bakery Case Opening and Closing Checklists
B4     : How to use this template:
B5     : ▸ Print the checklist for the right shift every day
B6     : ▸ The shift lead checks off each task as it is completed
B7     : ▸ At the end of the shift, sign off and hand over to the next shift lead
B9     : Customization:
B10    : ▸ Green cells are editable — adjust who is assigned and the times
B11    : ▸ Add tasks specific to your pastry kitchen in the empty rows at the bottom
B12    : ▸ Delete the tasks that don't apply to your shop
B14    : Mark ✓ in the "✓ Done" column (dropdown): that is the column that counts completed tasks.
B16    : Version 2.0 · October 2026 · aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates · info@aichef.pro

B2     : 02 · Pastry Kitchen Production Checklists: Doughs, Creams & Fillings, Decorating
B4     : Tasks organized by production area.
B5     : ▸ Each tab covers a specific area of the pastry kitchen
B6     : ▸ Tasks are in chronological order within the shift
B7     : ▸ Customize by adding your own kitchen's recipes and preparations
B9     : Mark ✓ in the "✓ Done" column (dropdown): that is the column that counts completed tasks.
B11    : Version 2.0 · October 2026 · aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates · info@aichef.pro

A5     :   Viennoiserie Doughs (Croissant, Brioche, Danish)
A6     : 1
B6     : Check the sourdough starter / preferment (smell, volume, texture)
C6     : Doubles in 3–4 h · lactic smell, never vinegary
D6     : Kitchen
E6     : Pastry cook
F6     : 5:00 AM
A7     : 2
B7     : Refresh the sourdough starter if needed (feed with flour and water)
C7     : Feed 1:1:1 · rest at 75–79 °F
D7     : Kitchen
E7     : Pastry cook
F7     : 5:00 AM
A8     : 3
B8     : Weigh ingredients for croissant dough (bread flour, butter, yeast)
C8     : Bread flour, 12.5–13.5% protein · European-style butter, 82% fat
D8     : Kitchen
E8     : Pastry cook
F8     : 5:15 AM
A9     : 4
B9     : Short mix in the planetary mixer (1st speed 3–4 min, 2nd speed 2–3 min): gluten finishes developing during lamination
C9     : 1st speed 3–4 min · 2nd speed 2–3 min
D9     : Kitchen
E9     : Pastry cook
F9     : 5:30 AM
A10    : 5
B10    : Check dough temperature at mixer exit (72–75 °F; 68–72 °F if it goes straight to the chill block)
C10    : 72–75 °F · 68–72 °F if it goes straight to chilling
D10    : Kitchen
E10    : Pastry cook
F10    : 5:45 AM
A11    : 6
B11    : Chill the dough block before laminating (blast chiller or walk-in)
C11    : 1 h in the blast chiller or 12 h in the walk-in at 37–39 °F
D11    : Kitchen
E11    : Pastry cook
F11    : 5:45 AM
A12    : 7
B12    : Take yesterday's shaped croissants out of the retarder and check the proof level
C12    : Overnight retard · ramps up to 75–79 °F · doubles in size
D12    : Kitchen
E12    : Pastry cook
F12    : 6:45 AM
A13    : 8
B13    : Egg wash and bake the first bake of the day for the case opening
C13    : 375–400 °F · 14–18 min · in the case before 8:00 AM
D13    : Kitchen
E13    : Pastry cook
F13    : 7:00 AM
A14    : 9
B14    : Laminate with butter (3 single folds or 2 double folds)
C14    : Dough and butter at 54–57 °F · final thickness 1/8 in (3–3.5 mm)
D14    : Kitchen
E14    : Pastry cook
F14    : 7:00 AM
A15    : 10
B15    : Cut and shape: one part for today's 2nd bake and the rest into the retarder for tomorrow
C15    : Croissant 2.3–2.5 oz (65–70 g) · pain au chocolat 2.5–2.6 oz (70–75 g)
D15    : Kitchen
E15    : Pastry cook
F15    : 7:30 AM
A16    : 11
B16    : Proof in the proofer at no more than 82 °F (above that the lamination butter melts)
C16    : 75–79 °F (82 °F max) · 75–80% RH · 2.5–3 h · doubles in size
D16    : Kitchen
E16    : Pastry cook
F16    : 7:45 AM
A17    : 12
B17    : Egg wash the 2nd bake before baking

D17    : Kitchen
E17    : Pastry assistant
F17    : 10:30 AM
A18    : 13

D18    : Kitchen
E18    : Pastry cook

=== Hoja «Instructions» · protegida=False · área= · títulos=None · paneles=None · papel=1 · orientación=portrait
    pie: AI Chef Pro · aichef.pro · Page &P of &N
B2     : 08 · Pastry Shop Opening & Closing Checklist (Whole Shop)
B4     : What this template covers:
B5     : ▸ The SHOP: sales floor, counter, bakery cases, register and entrances. Kitchen production goes in file 01.
B6     : ▸ Tasks are in the order they are actually done, each with a suggested due-by time.
B7     : ▸ The first task of the day is the bakery cases: turn them on at least 45 minutes before stocking, the time they need to reach 41 °F or below — the limit for TCS items such as cream-filled pastries, cheesecake and fillings (FDA Food Code 3-501.16). The schedule turns them on at 6:45 AM, stocks at 7:45 AM and opens to the public at 8:00 AM.
B8     : ▸ Closing starts with selling, not clearing: first group and sign the markdown product, at 7:50 PM lock the door to the public, and only then empty the bakery case.
B9     : ▸ The last four closing tasks stay where they are: final checks, trash, alarm and lock-up.
B11    : How to use this template:
B12    : ▸ Print the opening and closing sheets every day, or fill them in on a tablet.
B13    : ▸ The shift lead checks off each task as it is completed and signs off at the end.
B14    : ▸ Tasks that point to another file in the kit cite it by number: follow the reference.
B16    : Customization:
B17    : ▸ Green cells are editable: adjust who is assigned, times and notes to your shop.
B18    : ▸ At the bottom of each sheet there are five free rows for your own tasks; the counter already includes them.
B19    : ▸ Delete the tasks that don't apply to your shop (tasting area, coffee machine, delivery).
B21    : Mark ✓ in the "✓ Done" column: that is the column that counts completed tasks.
B23    : Questions or suggestions: info@aichef.pro — we reply within 24–48 business hours.
B24    : Designed by John Guerrero — chef and restaurant consultant since 2010, in professional kitchens since age 17 · johnguerrero.es
B25    : Version 2.0 · October 2026 · aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates · info@aichef.pro

# 09-cash-drawer-opening-closing.xlsx
# docProps: title='Cash Drawer Opening & Closing: Count Sheet, Z Report & Over/Short Log' · subject='Pastry Shop Checklists Kit Pro · v2.0' · keywords='pastry shop checklist, bakery opening checklist, bakery cleaning schedule, cake order form, bakery production schedule, bakery allergen chart, food temperature log, receiving log, AI Chef Pro' · description='aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates' · category='AI Chef Pro · Digital products' · creator='AI Chef Pro'

=== Hoja «Instructions» · protegida=False · área='Instructions'!$A$1:$A$20 · títulos=None · paneles=None · papel=1 · orientación=portrait
B2     : 09 · Cash Drawer Opening & Closing: Count Sheet, Z Report & Over/Short Log
B4     : What it solves:
B5     : ▸ The daily cash count for the shop register: starting bank, count by denomination and reconciliation against the POS Z report
B6     : ▸ The monthly log, to see at a glance which days were over/short and by how much
B8     : How to use:
B9     : ▸ Cash Drawer Opening: count the starting bank and enter it in the green cell "Starting bank (opening float)"
B10    : ▸ Cash Drawer Closing: print the POS Z report, count the cash by denomination and log card sales (WITHOUT tips), other payments and card tips paid out in cash from the drawer
B11    : ▸ The starting bank is subtracted automatically: TOTAL COLLECTED = (cash counted − starting bank) + card sales + other
B12    : ▸ If the OVER / SHORT box turns amber, the drawer doesn't balance: check it before closing
B13    : ▸ Monthly Cash Log: each day enter net cash (without the starting bank), card sales without tips, other payments plus tips paid out, and the Z report; the over/short calculates itself
B15    : Customization:
B16    : ▸ Green cells are editable: adjust amounts, assignments and times
B17    : ▸ Add or remove tasks to fit your routine and delete the denominations you don't use. The table uses US bills and coins (the penny is no longer minted but is still legal tender; a 25¢ roll counts $10 and a 10¢ roll $5). In the UK, Canada or Australia, change each row's label and value
B18    : ▸ Laminate a printed copy and use dry-erase markers to reuse it every day
B20    : Works with:
B21    : ▸ 08 Shop Opening & Closing — cashing out is one of its last tasks of the day
B22    : ▸ 10 Production Schedule & Waste Log — leftover case product is logged on its sheet "Made vs Sold"
B24    : Mark ✓ in the "✓ Done" column (dropdown) for each task you complete.
B26    : © AI Chef Pro — aichef.pro
B27    : Designed by John Guerrero — chef and restaurant consultant since 2010, in professional kitchens since age 17 · johnguerrero.es
B28    : Contact: info@aichef.pro
B30    : Version 2.0 · October 2026 · aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates · info@aichef.pro

# docProps: title='Bakery Production Schedule & Waste Log: Weekly Plan, Made vs. Sold & Summary' · subject='Pastry Shop Checklists Kit Pro · v2.0' · keywords='pastry shop checklist, bakery opening checklist, bakery cleaning schedule, cake order form, bakery production schedule, bakery allergen chart, food temperature log, receiving log, AI Chef Pro' · description='aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates' · category='AI Chef Pro · Digital products' · creator='AI Chef Pro'

=== Hoja «Instructions» · protegida=False · área= · títulos=None · paneles=None · papel=1 · orientación=portrait
    pie: AI Chef Pro · aichef.pro · Page &P of &N
B2     : 10 · Bakery Production Schedule & Waste Log: Weekly Plan, Made vs. Sold & Summary
B4     : What this template solves:
B5     : ▸ How much you make of each item, how much you sell and how much you throw away — with the cost in your currency.
B6     : ▸ Waste is the margin that slips away unseen: measuring it for one week already changes the plan.
B8     : How to use this template:
B9     : ▸ Sheet "Weekly Plan": enter the planned units for each day. This is the plan that goes down to the kitchen.
B10    : ▸ Sheet "Made vs Sold": when you close the shop, log what was made and sold of each item.
B11    : ▸ Leftover, waste percentage and its cost calculate themselves. Fill in the cost per unit from your actual recipe costing (if you don't have it, start with the ingredient cost).
B12    : ▸ Sheet "Summary by Category": see where waste is concentrated before touching the plan.
B14    : "Action" column: what to do with leftovers
B15    : ▸ Each option carries its condition inside. Read it before choosing: not all leftovers can be frozen.
B16    : ▸ Always log what you decide: it lets you defend your judgment in front of the health inspector.
B18    : Customization:
B19    : ▸ Green cells are editable. Replace the products with your own: the category is chosen from the list.
B20    : ▸ Each category in the Weekly Plan has two free rows, already included in the totals.
B21    : ▸ Kit references: the leftovers you log here come from the closing in file 08 (→ 08 Shop Opening & Closing · sheet "Shop Closing").
B24    : Questions or suggestions: info@aichef.pro — we reply within 24–48 business hours.
B25    : Designed by John Guerrero — chef and restaurant consultant since 2010, in professional kitchens since age 17 · johnguerrero.es
B26    : Version 2.0 · October 2026 · aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates · info@aichef.pro

Donate before you discard: the Bill Emerson Good Samaritan Food Donation Act (42 U.S.C. 1791) protects good-faith donations; also check your state and county rules.

=== Hoja «Instructions» · protegida=False · área= · títulos=None · paneles=None · papel=1 · orientación=portrait
    pie: AI Chef Pro · aichef.pro · Page &P of &N
B2     : 11 · Cake Order Form & Custom Order Log: Order Form, Log, Pickups & Today's Orders
B4     : What this template solves:
B5     : ▸ A badly recorded custom order is the most expensive complaint in a pastry shop: the cake is already made.
B6     : ▸ One order form per custom order, a monthly log for the money and a pickup schedule by time slot.
B8     : How to use this template:
B9     : ▸ Sheet "Order Form": print it and fill it in in front of the customer. Read the cake message and the pickup date back out loud before taking the deposit.
B10    : ▸ Sheet "Order Log": one line per order. The balance due calculates itself.
B11    : ▸ Sheet "Pickup Schedule": spread the orders across pickup slots so pickups don't pile up.
B12    : ▸ Sheet "Today's Orders": the one you hang in the kitchen every morning (→ 08 Shop Opening & Closing · sheet "Shop Opening").
B14    : Allergens:
B15    : ▸ If a customer declares a CONFIRMED allergy, tell the kitchen in writing and check every component (→ 12 Bakery Allergen Chart · sheet "Allergen Chart").
B17    : Privacy (kit guideline, not legal advice): this form collects personal data and HEALTH data (allergies). Before printing it, write the business name and contact in the green cell at the bottom. Collect only what you need. The allergy consent box is separate from the marketing one and neither may be pre-checked. Keep the forms locked up, not in view at the counter, and shred them one month after pickup, except what you need for taxes. Check your state's privacy law.
B19    : Customization:
B20    : ▸ Green cells are editable. The Status, Payment method and Order channel dropdowns are changed from Data › Data Validation.
B21    : ▸ Adapt the deposit policy to yours before printing the order form.
B24    : Questions or suggestions: info@aichef.pro — we reply within 24–48 business hours.
B25    : Designed by John Guerrero — chef and restaurant consultant since 2010, in professional kitchens since age 17 · johnguerrero.es
B26    : Version 2.0 · October 2026 · aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates · info@aichef.pro

# 12-bakery-allergen-chart.xlsx
# docProps: title='Bakery Allergen Chart (9 Major Allergens, UK 14): Matrix, Chart, Case Sign & Labels' · subject='Pastry Shop Checklists Kit Pro · v2.0' · keywords='pastry shop checklist, bakery opening checklist, bakery cleaning schedule, cake order form, bakery production schedule, bakery allergen chart, food temperature log, receiving log, AI Chef Pro' · description='aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates' · category='AI Chef Pro · Digital products' · creator='AI Chef Pro'

=== Hoja «Instructions» · protegida=False · área= · títulos=None · paneles=None · papel=1 · orientación=portrait
    pie: AI Chef Pro · aichef.pro · Page &P of &N
B2     : 12 · Bakery Allergen Chart (9 Major Allergens, UK 14): Matrix, Chart, Case Sign & Labels
B4     : This template is delivered as a DRAFT. The symbols come pre-marked in gray as a starting proposal. Do not publish or show anything without checking each item against your actual recipe and your suppliers' spec sheets. The business is responsible for the information.
B6     : What the law requires:
B7     : ▸ US law: the 9 major allergens (FALCPA + FASTER Act) on packaged food; on unpackaged items (bakery case, counter), written notice per the FDA Food Code 2022 3-602.12(C) where your state has adopted it. UK: 14 allergens (Food Information Regulations 2014).
B8     : ▸ For unpackaged products, the information must be IN WRITING and accessible before purchase (3-602.12(C): handout, sign, case label or menu).
B9     : ▸ A "may contain" statement is voluntary and must be truthful: it does not replace declaring the allergens you use on purpose.
B11    : How to use this template:
B12    : ▸ Sheet "Allergen Matrix": go through it item by item. Mark ● if the product contains it and ○ if it may contain traces through cross-contact in your kitchen.
B13    : ▸ Record the supplier and the spec sheet number backing each decision, enter the verification date and sign. Until you enter the date, the Status column says DO NOT PUBLISH.
B14    : ▸ The Verification notes column tells you what to check on items where the symbol depends on the brand or recipe: pain aux raisins and candied fruit (sulfites, declared at 10 ppm or more), almond nougat / torrone (egg white), chocolate coatings and chips (soy lecithin), polvorones (sesame and butter).
B15    : ▸ The cross-contact sentence on the sign and the chart is in a green cell: replace the list with the allergens actually handled in your kitchen. Declaring "all 9" (or "all 14") when you don't work with fish or shellfish is the generic "may contain" that doesn't help.
B16    : ▸ Sheet "Allergen Chart": it fills itself in from the matrix. It is the document you show the customer and the one the health inspector asks for. Print it when no item is left unverified.
B17    : ▸ Sheet "Case Sign": hang it in view of the customer, next to the bakery case.
B18    : ▸ Sheet "Case Labels": one label per item, with price and abbreviated allergens.
B20    : When to review it:
B21    : ▸ Whenever you change a recipe, an ingredient or a supplier.
B22    : ▸ At least once a year, even if nothing has changed, and always before a holiday campaign.
B24    : Customization:
B25    : ▸ Green cells are editable. The ● and ○ symbols are chosen from the dropdown in each cell.
B26    : ▸ There are four free rows at the bottom of the matrix for your own items.
B27    : ▸ Kit references: case labels go out at opening (→ 08 Shop Opening & Closing · sheet "Shop Opening") and the allergens declared by the customer are logged on the order form (→ 11 Cake Orders).
B30    : Questions or suggestions: info@aichef.pro — we reply within 24–48 business hours.
B31    : Designed by John Guerrero — chef and restaurant consultant since 2010, in professional kitchens since age 17 · johnguerrero.es
B32    : Version 2.0 · October 2026 · aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates · info@aichef.pro

# 13-food-temperature-log-receiving-log.xlsx
# docProps: title='Food Temperature Log, Receiving Log, Prep Labels & Shelf Life Chart' · subject='Pastry Shop Checklists Kit Pro · v2.0' · keywords='pastry shop checklist, bakery opening checklist, bakery cleaning schedule, cake order form, bakery production schedule, bakery allergen chart, food temperature log, receiving log, AI Chef Pro' · description='aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates' · category='AI Chef Pro · Digital products' · creator='AI Chef Pro'

=== Hoja «Instructions» · protegida=False · área= · títulos=None · paneles=None · papel=1 · orientación=portrait
    pie: AI Chef Pro · aichef.pro · Page &P of &N
B2     : 13 · Food Temperature Log, Receiving Log, Prep Labels & Shelf Life Chart
B4     : What this template solves:
B5     : ▸ The kit asks you to log temperatures in several checklists: this is the sheet where they are logged.
B6     : ▸ The temperature log, the receiving log and the prep labels are the three records a health inspector asks for in a pastry kitchen.
B8     : How to use this template:
B9     : ▸ Sheet "Temperature Log": one sheet per month. Two readings a day, morning and afternoon. Readings outside the target range turn red on their own: always write the corrective action.
B10    : ▸ Print one sheet per month and keep it signed. The records are proof that the control exists.
B11    : ▸ Sheet "Receiving Log": check the temperature before signing the invoice / packing slip. If you reject something, log the reason the same day.
B12    : ▸ Sheet "Prep Labels": fill in, print and stick on. Everything that goes into the walk-in comes out labeled with a date (date marking: ready-to-eat TCS food lasts 7 days at most at 41 °F, FDA Food Code 3-501.17), and anything made with egg also gets the TIME (a kit rule).
B13    : ▸ Sheet "Shelf Life": the reference for how long each preparation lasts. It is a guideline, including the egg rows: there the 24 hours are a kit quality target, not a legal limit. Ready-to-eat TCS food carries a date (date marking) and lasts 7 days at most at 41 °F, day 1 = the day made (FDA Food Code 3-501.17).
B15    : Starting target ranges:
B16    : ▸ Walk-in cooler 32 to 41 °F · dough retarder 36 to 41 °F · freezer 0 °F or below · bakery cases 32 to 41 °F · blast chiller 135 to 70 °F in 2 h and to 41 °F in 4 more h (FDA Food Code 3-501.14 and 3-501.16).
B17    : ▸ Adjust the ranges to your food safety plan from Home › Conditional Formatting › Manage Rules. The 41 °F limit for TCS food can be lowered, never raised: it is the FDA Food Code limit (3-501.16).
B19    : Customization:
B20    : ▸ Green cells are editable. Change the equipment names to your own.
B21    : ▸ Kit references: case readings come from opening and closing (→ 08 Shop Opening & Closing) and walk-in readings from the kitchen (→ 01 Opening & Closing · sheets "Kitchen Opening" and "Kitchen Closing").
B24    : Questions or suggestions: info@aichef.pro — we reply within 24–48 business hours.
B25    : Designed by John Guerrero — chef and restaurant consultant since 2010, in professional kitchens since age 17 · johnguerrero.es
B26    : Version 2.0 · October 2026 · aichef.pro/en/digital-products/pastry-shop-checklist-templates · info@aichef.pro

=== Hoja «Receiving Log» · protegida=False · área= · títulos=$4:$4 · paneles=A5 · papel=1 · orientación=landscape
    pie: AI Chef Pro · aichef.pro · Page &P of &N
A1     : Receiving Log
A2     : Month: ________________     Year: ________     One line per delivery. Check the temperature BEFORE signing the invoice.
A4     : Date
B4     : Supplier
C4     : Invoice #
D4     : Product
E4     : Lot #
F4     : Expiration date
G4     : Temp at delivery (°F)
H4     : Vehicle & packaging
I4     : Accepted
J4     : Reason for rejection
K4     : Initials
A35    : Deliveries logged
B35    = =COUNTIF(B5:B34,"?*")   → caché 0.0
C35    : Rejected
D35    = =COUNTIF(I5:I34,"Rejected")   → caché 0.0
A37    :   Rejection Criteria
A38    : Reject the delivery if: refrigerated TCS food arrives above 41 °F · shell eggs arrive in a truck with air above 45 °F · milk and cream arrive above 45 °F (allowed only where dairy law permits it and they are cooled to 41 °F within 4 h; FDA Food Code 3-202.11 and 3-501.14) · frozen food shows signs of thawing (frost, deformed block, liquid in the case) · the packaging is torn, open or wet · the expiration or best-by date is less than 2 days away · the truck is dirty or not cold · the invoice doesn't match what you receive. Log the reason, take a photo and notify the supplier the same day. Accepted with note: the goods are received, but the issue is written on the invoice before you sign it and photographed; the product is set aside, labeled, and not used until the supplier replies.
A42    : Measure the delivery temperature with a probe thermometer between two packages, never by piercing the product.
A44    : — Pastry Shop Checklists Kit Pro · AI Chef Pro · aichef.pro

B11    : 32 to 41 °F
C11    : 24 h
D11    : Kit target 24 h (eggs) unless a shelf-life study shows more; legal max 7 days at 41 °F (3-501.17). In a sanitized jar
A12    : Simple syrup
B12    : 32 to 41 °F
C12    : 15 days
D12    : High sugar content: lasts longer
A13    : Cut fruit
B13    : 32 to 41 °F
C13    : 24 h
D13    : With gelatin or glaze protection
A14    : Raw laminated dough
B14    : 0 °F or below
C14    : 1 month
D14    : Freeze shaped and wrapped
A15    : Sponge cake layers
B15    : 0 °F or below
C15    : 3 months
D15    : Wrapped individually
A16    : Assembled whipped-cream cake
B16    : 32 to 41 °F
C16    : 24 h
D16    : Cream-filled: case at 41 °F or below (FDA Food Code 3-501.16)
A17    : Baked viennoiserie
B17    : Room temperature, in the case
C17    : Same day
D17    : Next day: day-old discount or waste. Cream-filled: case at 41 °F or below (3-501.16)
A18    : Baked bread
B18    : Room temperature
C18    : 24–48 h
D18    : Depends on size and crust
A24    : In the egg-based rows, the 24 hours are a kit quality target, not a legal limit. Ready-to-eat TCS food carries a date (date marking) and lasts 7 days at most at 41 °F, day 1 = the day made (FDA Food Code 3-501.17); filled items are held in the bakery case at 41 °F or below (3-501.16). In all other rows the shelf life is a GUIDELINE and assumes correct preparation, rapid cooling, clean packaging and an unbroken cold chain: you set it, and it must be in your food safety plan. If in doubt about a specific preparation, ask your food safety advisor or your health department, or commission a shelf-life study.
A28    : — Pastry Shop Checklists Kit Pro · AI Chef Pro · aichef.pro
